I just quit caring about the things that usually settle me down. I told you about the fishing, right? And my records, tailgating, the home-brew... it all just feels like a chore now. Like the home-brew, I used to love checking the specific gravity, making sure the fermentation was right on track. I had this glass hydrometer, real delicate thing. You have to spin it in the sample tube so the bubbles don't stick to the side and mess up the reading. If you get a bad reading, you might bottle it too early, and then you get bottle bombs. That happened to my neighbor, Dave, a few years back. He was making this jalapeño IPA, which sounds terrible anyway, but he bottled it in these cheap flip-top bottles he bought online. Middle of the night, sounded like firecrackers going off in his garage. His wife was furious. She made him clean it all up, and the whole place smelled like spicy stale beer for a month. Dave ended up selling all his equipment at a yard sale. I almost bought his carboy, but it had a little chip on the rim, and you don't want to mess with compromised glass when it's holding five gallons of liquid. \n\nBut yeah, to answer your question, no. None of it sounds fun anymore. Just too much effort.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're moving or acting slower than usual?\n\nPatient: Slower? I don't... no, I haven't really noticed being slow. I mean, I'm on my feet all day at the store, so I'm moving around the same as always, I guess. The floors there are solid concrete, though, so it takes a toll on your knees no matter how fast you walk. They polished all the aisles last year, brought in these massive industrial buffer machines. They had to do it overnight so it wouldn't bother the customers, but man, the smell of that sealing compound they used lingered for a whole week. Smelled exactly like model airplane glue. \n\nI used to build those little Revell models when I was a kid. P-51 Mustangs, B-17 bombers... the decals were always the hardest part. You had to soak them in a bowl of warm water for exactly the right amount of time, or they'd fold over on themselves and you'd completely ruin the star insignia. My mom hated when I built them on the kitchen table because I always managed to get a drop of glue on the vinyl tablecloth. It had this ugly yellow floral pattern anyway, so I don't know why she cared so much. I think she bought it at Sears. You remember when Sears used to have that massive catalog? It was like a phone book. You could buy a whole house out of that thing back in the day, just order it and they'd send you the lumber on a train. \n\nBut yeah, I don't think I'm acting slower or anything. Just regular speed.\n\nDoctor: Do you often feel like you're worthless or that you blame yourself for things that happen?\n\nPatient: Worthless? No, I wouldn't say that. I mean, I do my job, I pay my bills. Blaming myself... not really. I mean, things just happen, you know? Like last week at the store, they had this huge display of those new LED work lights. Supposed to be super bright, like 5,000 lumens or something crazy. Anyway, some kid came running down the aisle and knocked the whole endcap over. Lights everywhere. The plastic packaging on those things is ridiculous, by the way. You need a chainsaw just to open them. Clamshell packaging, they call it. I cut my hand on one of those clamshells trying to open a pair of pliers a few months ago. Bled all over the counter. The manager made me fill out an incident report, like it was my fault the plastic was sharper than the actual pliers. \n\nThey use that same plastic for everything now. Batteries, scissors... you buy scissors, and you need scissors just to get them out of the package. It makes no sense. My grandfather used to have this old pair of heavy metal shears. Cast iron handles, heavy as a brick. He used them for everything—cutting wire, trimming the hedges, opening boxes. He kept them in this old leather tool belt that smelled like motor oil and peppermint. He always had peppermint candies in his pocket. The soft ones, not the hard discs. \n\nBut no, I don't sit around feeling worthless or blaming myself for stuff. It's just... life, you know?\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're having trouble getting motivated to do things that you normally do?\n\nPatient: Motivated... well, I don't know if it's motivation exactly. I mean, I go to work, I get up, but... I guess I'm just not doing the extra stuff. Like I said, the brewing and the records. But the everyday stuff, I'm still doing it. Barely, sometimes. I had to drag the trash cans out to the curb this morning, and the wheels on the recycling bin are completely shot. They get that grit in them from the road salt they put down in the winter, and it just grinds away at the plastic axle until it won't spin anymore. \n\nThe city is supposed to replace them if they break, but you have to call this hotline, and you're just on hold forever listening to this terrible elevator music. It sounds like a Casio keyboard from the 80s. My brother had one of those keyboards. He thought he was going to be the next Eddie Van Halen, but he only ever learned how to play the opening riff to \"Smoke on the Water.\" Over and over again, all summer long. Drove my dad absolutely up the wall. Dad finally took the batteries out and hid them in the garage behind his cans of WD-40. WD-40 is great, but people use it wrong, you know? It's a water displacer, not a true lubricant. If you really want to grease a squeaky hinge, you need white lithium grease. It comes in this little tube, looks just like toothpaste. \n\nBut yeah, motivation... I don't know. I'm just doing what I have to do to get by, I guess. It's just hard to care about the rest of it.\n\nDoctor: Are there any small things that you still find pleasure in or enjoy doing?\n\nPatient: Pleasure in small things? I mean... not really. That's the thing. I quit caring about the things that usually settle me down, you know? Like fishing, digging through vinyl records, tailgating, or checking on my home-brew. They all just felt like chores lately. I haven't even looked at my brewing setup in weeks. \n\nI used to love making this pale ale, used Cascade hops. You have to be careful with hops, though, because if you boil them too long, they get too bitter. My buddy Dave taught me that. Dave's a smart guy, works in HVAC. He came over last summer to fix my AC unit when it went out during that heatwave. Turns out it was just a blown capacitor. It's a little silver cylinder, looks like a tiny soda can. He swapped it out in ten minutes and only charged me for the part. \n\nHVAC guys make a killing, though. I should have learned a trade like that. Instead, I've been working retail for thirty years. You stand on that concrete all day, and it just ruins your back. I bought these expensive orthotic insoles, the kind you have to bake in the oven so they mold to your feet. They smelled terrible when I was heating them up, like burning tires. My wife made me open all the windows, and the pollen was so bad that week everything in the house got coated in this fine yellow dust. \n\nBut yeah, the brewing, the records... I just don't have the energy for it. It all just feels like work now.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're resting or sleeping more than usual?\n\nPatient: Sleeping more? I wish. Honestly, it's the opposite. Even when I do manage to fall asleep now, I wake up way before daylight. Pitch black outside, and my stomach is just tied in knots. My mind is already racing, thinking about bills and everything I've messed up lately. \n\nIt's so quiet at that time of morning, you know? You can hear every little creak in the house. The refrigerator kicks on, and it sounds like a jet engine. It's an older model, one of those side-by-sides with the ice maker in the door. The ice maker's been broken for five years, though. It just hums and clicks, but nothing comes out. I tried fixing it once, watched a bunch of videos online. They make it look so easy. \"Just unscrew this panel and replace the solenoid valve.\" Yeah, right. I ended up dropping a screw down the back of the freezer compartment, and it rattled around in the defrost fan for a week before I finally got it out with a magnet on a stick. \n\nMagnets are fascinating, really. My grandson has these magnetic building tiles, Magna-Tiles I think they're called. He builds these huge towers, and then just smashes them down. The noise drives my wife crazy, but he loves it. Kids have so much energy. I don't know where they get it. \n\nBut yeah, sleep... I'm definitely not getting more of it. Just lying there in the dark, stressing out before the sun even comes up.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like your movements or speech have slowed down or sped up compared to your normal pace?\n\nPatient: Slowed down? Or sped up? I don't... I don't really know. I haven't noticed anything like that, to be honest. I mean, my wife always tells me I talk too loud when I get going, but that's just because I spent so many years working in the stockroom before they moved me out to the floor. The conveyor belts back there are incredibly loud. It's this constant metal-on-metal screeching sound. \n\nThey finally replaced the rollers a couple of years ago, but before that, it was unbearable. The guy who came to fix them brought this huge toolbox, one of those red metal ones with all the drawers. Snap-on, I think. Those tools are so expensive. A single wrench can cost you fifty bucks. But I guess if you're using them every day, you want quality. Like shoes. I was telling you about those insoles... if you don't take care of your feet, your whole body pays for it. \n\nBut as for moving slow or fast... I don't think so. I'm just moving like I normally do, I guess. Nothing feels different about that.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like your body is heavier or harder to move than usual?\n\nPatient: Heavier? No, I wouldn't say that. I don't feel like I'm dragging weights around or anything. I mean, my knees ache, but that's just getting older, right? Fifty-four years of gravity taking its toll. \n\nGravity is a funny thing when you think about it. It just constantly pulls you down. I saw this documentary once about astronauts on the International Space Station, and how their muscles just waste away because they don't have gravity working against them. They have to strap themselves to these special treadmills with bungee cords just to get a workout in. Imagine running strapped down like that. Must chafe terribly. \n\nI bought a treadmill a few years ago, thinking I'd use it in the winter when it's too cold to walk outside. Found it on Craigslist for fifty bucks. The guy who sold it to me lived in this massive house out in the suburbs, had a three-car garage and everything. His driveway was paved with those stamped concrete blocks that look like cobblestones. I always wondered how they keep the weeds from growing up between all those cracks. My neighbor uses this vinegar and dish soap mixture on his driveway, swears it works better than Roundup. Plus, it's safer for the dogs. We used to have a golden retriever, Buster. Best dog in the world, but he chewed through the garden hose twice. \n\nBut no, my body doesn't feel heavier or harder to move. Just the usual aches and pains, I guess.\n\nDoctor: Have you had periods like this before where you felt similarly, or is this the first time?\n\nPatient: Periods like this? You mean... feeling like this? I don't know, maybe? It's hard to remember. I feel like I've always been a worrier, you know? Like my mind is always going. But this bad... I'm not sure. \n\nMy memory isn't what it used to be. I was trying to remember the name of this actor the other day, the one who was in that movie with the boat... not Titanic, the other one. The one with the giant shark. Jaws! Right. Richard Dreyfuss. I loved him in Mr. Holland's Opus. Such a good movie. We had it on VHS. Remember VHS tapes? You had to rewind them before you took them back to Blockbuster or they'd charge you a fee. \"Be kind, rewind.\" My kids don't even know what a VHS tape is. Everything is streaming now. Netflix, Hulu, Disney Plus... it's too much. We pay more for all these subscriptions than we ever did for cable. \n\nI tried to cancel our cable last month, actually. Called the company, and they kept transferring me to different departments. \"Retention specialists,\" they call them. They just read from a script and offer you a discount for six months, hoping you'll forget to cancel again when the price goes back up. It's a racket, really. Just like those extended warranties they try to sell you at the electronics store. Complete waste of money. \n\nBut yeah... have I felt like this before? I honestly couldn't tell you. It all just kind of blends together after a while.\n\nDoctor: How long have you been feeling this low, is it just these three months or has it been longer?\n\nPatient: Three months. Yeah, it's been about three months. Ever since that... that crash, you know? Just this low spell hanging on since then. \n\nIt's funny how time works, though. Three months can feel like a lifetime or just a blink. Like when I was working the holiday rush at the store last year. November and December just evaporated. Poof. Gone. We were selling these remote-control drones, right? They were the hot item. Every parent came in looking for one, and we kept running out of stock. The shipping delays were a nightmare. The manager, Gary—he's this guy who wears those ties with the Looney Tunes characters on them, never quite matched his shirts—Gary was pulling his hair out. He actually has a bald spot right on the crown of his head that he tries to cover up, but the fluorescent lights in the store just bounce right off it. \n\nFluorescent lighting is terrible for you, by the way. I read an article that said it messes with your circadian rhythm. That's why I swapped all the bulbs in our house to those warm LED ones. Cost a small fortune, but they last forever. Supposedly. Although I had one burn out in the hallway fixture after just a month. Took it right back to the hardware store with the receipt. You always have to keep the receipt. My dad taught me that. He had this old shoebox in his closet where he kept every receipt for everything he ever bought. Even a pack of gum. \n\nBut yeah, it's been about three months of feeling this low. Since the crash.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. My wife made me open all the windows, and the pollen was so bad that week everything in the house got coated in this fine yellow dust. \n\nBut yeah, the brewing, the records... I just don't have the energy for it. It all just feels like work now.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're resting or sleeping more than usual?\n\nPatient: Sleeping more? I wish. My mind is already racing, thinking about bills and everything I've messed up lately. \n\nIt's so quiet at that time of morning, you know? You can hear every little creak in the house. The refrigerator kicks on, and it sounds like a jet engine. It's an older model, one of those side-by-sides with the ice maker in the door. The ice maker's been broken for five years, though. It just hums and clicks, but nothing comes out. I tried fixing it once, watched a bunch of videos online. They make it look so easy. \"Just unscrew this panel and replace the solenoid valve.\" Yeah, right. I ended up dropping a screw down the back of the freezer compartment, and it rattled around in the defrost fan for a week before I finally got it out with a magnet on a stick. \n\nMagnets are fascinating, really. Kids have so much energy. I don't know where they get it. \n\nBut yeah, sleep... I'm definitely not getting more of it. Just lying there in the dark, stressing out before the sun even comes up.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like your movements or speech have slowed down or sped up compared to your normal pace?\n\nPatient: Slowed down? The conveyor belts back there are incredibly loud. It's this constant metal-on-metal screeching sound. \n\nThey finally replaced the rollers a couple of years ago, but before that, it was unbearable. The guy who came to fix them brought this huge toolbox, one of those red metal ones with all the drawers. Snap-on, I think. Those tools are so expensive. A single wrench can cost you fifty bucks. I saw this documentary once about astronauts on the International Space Station, and how their muscles just waste away because they don't have gravity working against them. They have to strap themselves to these special treadmills with bungee cords just to get a workout in. Imagine running strapped down like that. First time? You know, I... I don't really know. Like I said before, it's hard to remember. It all kind of blends together. I mean, I've had times where I felt down, sure. Who hasn't? But this low? I'm not sure. 

It's like trying to remember what the weather was like on this exact day five years ago. You just can't do it unless you wrote it down. My wife, she keeps these little daily planner books. Has a stack of them in the bottom drawer of her nightstand going back to like 1998. She writes everything down. Dentist appointments, when she watered the houseplants, what we had for dinner. Last week she made this meatloaf with a brown sugar glaze on top. It was okay, but I prefer the classic ketchup glaze. That's how my mom used to make it. She'd mix a little mustard into the ketchup before she spread it on. Gives it a nice tang, you know? 

Mustard is one of those things, people either love it or hate it. Like mayonnaise. I can't stand mayonnaise. The texture is just wrong. I went to this deli once and specifically asked for no mayo on my turkey sandwich, and what do they do? Slathered it on both pieces of bread. I had to scrape it off with a plastic knife. Those plastic knives are useless, though. They bend if you press too hard. You can't even cut a pickle with them. 

But yeah, feeling this low before... I really couldn't say for sure. Maybe? Or maybe this is the first time it's been this bad. I just don't know.

Ran great for a decade until the transmission blew on the highway. I had to wait three hours for a tow truck in the freezing cold. I just... I quit caring about the things that usually settle me down. Like fishing, or digging through old vinyl records, tailgating, checking on my home-brew... it all just feels like chores now. \n\nWhich is a shame, because I had this really good amber ale fermenting not too long ago. But I actually had to dump the whole five-gallon batch down the drain. I must have messed up the sanitation step somewhere. It's real easy to do if you don't use that Star San liquid exactly right. There's this guy at the brewing supply shop over on 5th—tall guy, always wears a wool beanie, even when it's ninety degrees out—and he warned me about that. He said you gotta soak those plastic transfer tubes for at least five minutes. The parking in that lot is a total nightmare anyway, they share it with a dry cleaner and a dentist office. \n\nBut... yeah, I don't know. What was I saying? Oh, right, enjoying things. Even if I made a perfect batch of beer right now, I don't think I'd even care to drink it. Everything is just too much effort.\n\nDoctor: Are there any activities that you still find pleasure in or look forward to?\n\nPatient: Well, like I said, not really. I just... I quit caring about all of it. Fishing, looking through my old vinyls, tailgating before the games, even my home-brew... it all just feels like chores now. I don't look forward to any of it. \n\nWhich is weird, because tailgating used to be the highlight of my week. We'd always get the same spot in the south lot, right near the stadium entrance. My buddy Mike has this incredible portable grill, one of those Weber ones with the cast iron grates. He bought it at a garage sale for like twenty bucks, which is a steal, honestly. The guy selling it didn't even know what he had. It was at this house over in the Heights, you know, where all those big old Victorian homes are? Some of those houses still have the original stained glass windows. I always wondered how much it costs to fix one of those if it breaks. You can't just go to a hardware store for that. You'd probably have to hire a specialist, maybe even someone from out of state. \n\nBut yeah... no. Nothing really sounds good to me right now. It's all just... work.\n\nDoctor: Do you often feel nervous or on edge?\n\nPatient: I wouldn't say I'm nervous, really. Not... not on edge. Just tired, mostly. Though speaking of being on edge, my neighbor's dog has been barking at all hours lately. It's this little terrier mix, wire-haired thing. They leave him out in the side yard, the one with the chain-link fence. That fence has been rusting out at the bottom for years. I told him he ought to spray some rust converter on it, you know, the kind that turns black when it cures? You can get it in a spray can at the auto parts store. I used it on the frame of my old truck once. Worked like a charm. But the auto parts store I used to go to closed down last year. They turned it into one of those fancy coffee shops where they charge six bucks for a cup of black coffee. Can you believe that? Six bucks. And they don't even have regular cream, just oat milk or almond milk or whatever. \n\nBut no, I don't feel nervous. Just... heavy, I guess. Like everything is dragging me down.\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself moving or speaking more slowly than you used to?\n\nPatient: I don't really think so. I haven't noticed myself talking slower or anything. If anything, it feels like everyone else is just rushing around talking way too fast. Especially at work. I'm in retail, I work over at the hardware store on Route 9, mostly covering the plumbing and electrical aisles. Had a guy come in Tuesday talking a mile a minute about needing a P-trap for his guest bathroom. He wanted the cheap PVC one, but I always tell people the brass ones hold up a lot better if you're redoing the whole vanity. We sell this one brand that comes in these heavy green boxes. The delivery guy who brings them always parks his truck right in the fire lane out front. I have no idea how he hasn't gotten a ticket yet. The parking enforcement downtown is absolutely ruthless. This one meter maid, she wrote me a fifty-dollar ticket last year because my rear tire was maybe an inch over the painted line. And it was a faded line, too! You could barely even see the yellow paint because the city hasn't repainted that lot since maybe 2015. They were supposed to do it last summer, but they repaved the street behind the library instead. \n\nBut... no. I'm not moving slow. Just normal, I guess. Just tired.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any significant changes in your weight lately?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I guess my jeans are definitely looser. I had to pull my belt up an extra notch the other day. They used to have a whole wall of them, sorted by wash and everything. Now that space is just empty. Probably used the cheap lightweight mud instead of the all-purpose stuff. \n\nMy buddy Mike and I used to go to the food court there sometimes on our lunch breaks, get those giant slices of pizza. The pizza place had this guy working there who could toss the dough like three feet in the air. He said he learned it working at some fancy Italian place in Chicago. Chicago's supposed to be famous for deep dish, but I don't know, it always seemed more like a casserole to me than a pizza. You gotta eat it with a fork and knife, and that just defeats the whole purpose of a slice, you know? \n\nBut anyway... yeah, I'm losing weight. Most days lately I actually have to remind myself to eat at all. When I get home, supper just sits there looking like way too much trouble. So I usually just don't bother.\n\nDoctor: Do you often feel guilty about things that have happened?\n\nPatient: Guilty? I don't... I don't think so. Not really guilty. I mean, sure, I feel bad when I mess something up, like everyone does. Like last week, I accidentally knocked over a display of those little brass wood screws at the store. The ones they use for cabinet hinges. They went everywhere. It took me and the new kid, Jason, almost an hour to sweep them all up. Jason's a good kid, but he's always on his phone. He's got one of those massive ones, the Pro Max or whatever they call it. I don't get why people need phones the size of a tablet these days. My old flip phone works just fine. It's an LG, got it back in... man, maybe 2018? The battery still holds a charge for three days. You can't say that about these new ones. My sister, she has to plug hers in twice a day. She bought this special charging cable that lights up blue when it's plugged in. Looks like a neon sign in her living room. She got it at that discount store out by the highway, the one that used to be a roller rink back in the eighties. We used to go there every Friday night when we were kids. They played all that disco stuff, had the big mirror ball and everything. \n\nBut no, I wouldn't say I sit around feeling guilty about things. Just tired.\n\nDoctor: Are there any small things that still bring you some joy?\n\nPatient: Joy? No, not really. I don't know, I just... I quit caring about the things that usually settle me down. Like, I used to love fishing. Taking the boat out on the lake early in the morning. My buddy Mike and I, we'd go out to Miller's Pond. He's got this aluminum John boat, maybe fourteen feet long. We painted it this dark olive green last spring because the glare off the bare aluminum was blinding when the sun came up. We used this special marine-grade paint, supposed to be rust-proof and all that. Bought it at the hardware store, actually, with my employee discount. You wouldn't believe the markup on that stuff if you buy it at a regular marina. Those places rob you blind. Like this one place over by the reservoir, they wanted twenty bucks for a tiny little jar of salmon eggs for bait. Twenty bucks! You could buy a whole salmon at the grocery store for that. The grocery store near my house, they just remodeled the seafood section. Put in all these fancy curved glass cases. It looks nice, I guess, but it takes up half the aisle now, so you can barely get two carts past each other. And people just leave their carts sitting sideways right in the middle while they stare at the lobster tank. \n\nBut anyway, yeah. Fishing, digging through my old vinyl records, tailgating before the game, even checking on my home-brew in the basement... they all just feel like chores now. Too much effort. So I just don't do them.\n\nDoctor: Are there any activities that you still enjoy or find pleasure in?\n\nPatient: I think... didn't we just talk about this? I mean, like I was saying, no. I just quit caring about the things that usually settle me down. I told you about the fishing, right? And my records, tailgating, the home-brew... it all just feels like a chore now. Like the home-brew, I used to love checking the specific gravity, making sure the fermentation was right on track. I had this glass hydrometer, real delicate thing. You have to spin it in the sample tube so the bubbles don't stick to the side and mess up the reading. If you get a bad reading, you might bottle it too early, and then you get bottle bombs. That happened to my neighbor, Dave, a few years back. He was making this jalapeño IPA, which sounds terrible anyway, but he bottled it in these cheap flip-top bottles he bought online. Middle of the night, sounded like firecrackers going off in his garage. His wife was furious. She made him clean it all up, and the whole place smelled like spicy stale beer for a month. Dave ended up selling all his equipment at a yard sale. I almost bought his carboy, but it had a little chip on the rim, and you don't want to mess with compromised glass when it's holding five gallons of liquid. \n\nBut yeah, to answer your question, no. None of it sounds fun anymore. Just too much effort.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're moving or acting slower than usual?\n\nPatient: Slower? I don't... no, I haven't really noticed being slow. I mean, I'm on my feet all day at the store, so I'm moving around the same as always, I guess. The floors there are solid concrete, though, so it takes a toll on your knees no matter how fast you walk. They polished all the aisles last year, brought in these massive industrial buffer machines. They had to do it overnight so it wouldn't bother the customers, but man, the smell of that sealing compound they used lingered for a whole week. My mom hated when I built them on the kitchen table because I always managed to get a drop of glue on the vinyl tablecloth. Supposed to be super bright, like 5,000 lumens or something crazy. Anyway, some kid came running down the aisle and knocked the whole endcap over. Lights everywhere. He kept them in this old leather tool belt that smelled like motor oil and peppermint. He always had peppermint candies in his pocket. It comes in this little tube, looks just like toothpaste. \n\nBut yeah, motivation... I don't know. I'm just doing what I have to do to get by, I guess. He swapped it out in ten minutes and only charged me for the part. \n\nHVAC guys make a killing, though. I should have learned a trade like that. Instead, I've been working retail for thirty years. The parking in that lot is a total nightmare anyway, they share it with a dry cleaner and a dentist office. \n\nBut... yeah, I don't know. What was I saying? Oh, right, enjoying things. Everything is just too much effort.\n\nDoctor: Are there any activities that you still find pleasure in or look forward to?\n\nPatient: Well, like I said, not really. I just... I quit caring about all of it. Fishing, looking through my old vinyls, tailgating before the games, even my home-brew... it all just feels like chores now. I don't look forward to any of it. \n\nWhich is weird, because tailgating used to be the highlight of my week. We'd always get the same spot in the south lot, right near the stadium entrance. My buddy Mike has this incredible portable grill, one of those Weber ones with the cast iron grates. He bought it at a garage sale for like twenty bucks, which is a steal, honestly. The guy selling it didn't even know what he had. It was at this house over in the Heights, you know, where all those big old Victorian homes are? Some of those houses still have the original stained glass windows. I always wondered how much it costs to fix one of those if it breaks. You can't just go to a hardware store for that. You'd probably have to hire a specialist, maybe even someone from out of state. \n\nBut yeah... no. Nothing really sounds good to me right now. It's all just... work.\n\nDoctor: Do you often feel nervous or on edge?\n\nPatient: I wouldn't say I'm nervous, really. Not... not on edge. Just tired, mostly. Though speaking of being on edge, my neighbor's dog has been barking at all hours lately. It's this little terrier mix, wire-haired thing. They leave him out in the side yard, the one with the chain-link fence. That fence has been rusting out at the bottom for years. I told him he ought to spray some rust converter on it, you know, the kind that turns black when it cures? You can get it in a spray can at the auto parts store. I used it on the frame of my old truck once. Worked like a charm. But the auto parts store I used to go to closed down last year. They turned it into one of those fancy coffee shops where they charge six bucks for a cup of black coffee. Can you believe that? Six bucks. And they don't even have regular cream, just oat milk or almond milk or whatever. \n\nBut no, I don't feel nervous. Just... heavy, I guess. Like everything is dragging me down.\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself moving or speaking more slowly than you used to?\n\nPatient: I don't really think so. I haven't noticed myself talking slower or anything. If anything, it feels like everyone else is just rushing around talking way too fast. Especially at work. I'm in retail, I work over at the hardware store on Route 9, mostly covering the plumbing and electrical aisles. Had a guy come in Tuesday talking a mile a minute about needing a P-trap for his guest bathroom. He wanted the cheap PVC one, but I always tell people the brass ones hold up a lot better if you're redoing the whole vanity. We sell this one brand that comes in these heavy green boxes. The delivery guy who brings them always parks his truck right in the fire lane out front. I have no idea how he hasn't gotten a ticket yet. The parking enforcement downtown is absolutely ruthless. This one meter maid, she wrote me a fifty-dollar ticket last year because my rear tire was maybe an inch over the painted line. And it was a faded line, too! You could barely even see the yellow paint because the city hasn't repainted that lot since maybe 2015. They were supposed to do it last summer, but they repaved the street behind the library instead. \n\nBut... no. I'm not moving slow. Just normal, I guess. Just tired.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any significant changes in your weight lately?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I guess my jeans are definitely looser. I had to pull my belt up an extra notch the other day. I usually wear those Levi's 505s, the straight leg ones. You can't really find the good ones locally anymore, not since the big department store at the mall closed down. They used to have a whole wall of them, sorted by wash and everything. Now that space is just empty. They boarded it up with this ugly white drywall, and they didn't even bother to tape and mud the seams right. You can see the joint compound cracking already. Whoever the mall hired to do it was definitely cutting corners. Probably used the cheap lightweight mud instead of the all-purpose stuff. \n\nMy buddy Mike and I used to go to the food court there sometimes on our lunch breaks, get those giant slices of pizza. The pizza place had this guy working there who could toss the dough like three feet in the air. He said he learned it working at some fancy Italian place in Chicago. Chicago's supposed to be famous for deep dish, but I don't know, it always seemed more like a casserole to me than a pizza. You gotta eat it with a fork and knife, and that just defeats the whole purpose of a slice, you know? \n\nBut anyway... yeah, I'm losing weight. Most days lately I actually have to remind myself to eat at all. When I get home, supper just sits there looking like way too much trouble. So I usually just don't bother.\n\nDoctor: Do you often feel guilty about things that have happened?\n\nPatient: Guilty? I don't... I don't think so. Not really guilty. I mean, sure, I feel bad when I mess something up, like everyone does. Like last week, I accidentally knocked over a display of those little brass wood screws at the store. The ones they use for cabinet hinges. They went everywhere. It took me and the new kid, Jason, almost an hour to sweep them all up. Jason's a good kid, but he's always on his phone. He's got one of those massive ones, the Pro Max or whatever they call it. I don't get why people need phones the size of a tablet these days. My old flip phone works just fine. It's an LG, got it back in... man, maybe 2018? The battery still holds a charge for three days. You can't say that about these new ones. My sister, she has to plug hers in twice a day. She bought this special charging cable that lights up blue when it's plugged in. Looks like a neon sign in her living room. She got it at that discount store out by the highway, the one that used to be a roller rink back in the eighties. We used to go there every Friday night when we were kids. They played all that disco stuff, had the big mirror ball and everything. \n\nBut no, I wouldn't say I sit around feeling guilty about things. Just tired.\n\nDoctor: Are there any small things that still bring you some joy?\n\nPatient: Joy? No, not really. I don't know, I just... I quit caring about the things that usually settle me down. Like, I used to love fishing. Taking the boat out on the lake early in the morning. My buddy Mike and I, we'd go out to Miller's Pond. He's got this aluminum John boat, maybe fourteen feet long. We painted it this dark olive green last spring because the glare off the bare aluminum was blinding when the sun came up. We used this special marine-grade paint, supposed to be rust-proof and all that. Bought it at the hardware store, actually, with my employee discount. You wouldn't believe the markup on that stuff if you buy it at a regular marina. Those places rob you blind. Like this one place over by the reservoir, they wanted twenty bucks for a tiny little jar of salmon eggs for bait. Twenty bucks! You could buy a whole salmon at the grocery store for that. The grocery store near my house, they just remodeled the seafood section. Put in all these fancy curved glass cases. It looks nice, I guess, but it takes up half the aisle now, so you can barely get two carts past each other. And people just leave their carts sitting sideways right in the middle while they stare at the lobster tank. \n\nBut anyway, yeah. Fishing, digging through my old vinyl records, tailgating before the game, even checking on my home-brew in the basement... they all just feel like chores now. Too much effort. So I just don't do them.\n\nDoctor: Are there any activities that you still enjoy or find pleasure in?\n\nPatient: I think... didn't we just talk about this? I mean, like I was saying, no. I just quit caring about the things that usually settle me down. I told you about the fishing, right? And my records, tailgating, the home-brew... it all just feels like a chore now. Like the home-brew, I used to love checking the specific gravity, making sure the fermentation was right on track. I had this glass hydrometer, real delicate thing. You have to spin it in the sample tube so the bubbles don't stick to the side and mess up the reading. If you get a bad reading, you might bottle it too early, and then you get bottle bombs. That happened to my neighbor, Dave, a few years back. He was making this jalapeño IPA, which sounds terrible anyway, but he bottled it in these cheap flip-top bottles he bought online. Middle of the night, sounded like firecrackers going off in his garage. His wife was furious. She made him clean it all up, and the whole place smelled like spicy stale beer for a month. Dave ended up selling all his equipment at a yard sale. I almost bought his carboy, but it had a little chip on the rim, and you don't want to mess with compromised glass when it's holding five gallons of liquid. \n\nBut yeah, to answer your question, no. None of it sounds fun anymore. Just too much effort.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're moving or acting slower than usual?\n\nPatient: Slower? I don't... no, I haven't really noticed being slow. I mean, I'm on my feet all day at the store, so I'm moving around the same as always, I guess. The floors there are solid concrete, though, so it takes a toll on your knees no matter how fast you walk. They polished all the aisles last year, brought in these massive industrial buffer machines. They had to do it overnight so it wouldn't bother the customers, but man, the smell of that sealing compound they used lingered for a whole week. Smelled exactly like model airplane glue. \n\nI used to build those little Revell models when I was a kid. P-51 Mustangs, B-17 bombers... the decals were always the hardest part. You had to soak them in a bowl of warm water for exactly the right amount of time, or they'd fold over on themselves and you'd completely ruin the star insignia. My mom hated when I built them on the kitchen table because I always managed to get a drop of glue on the vinyl tablecloth. It had this ugly yellow floral pattern anyway, so I don't know why she cared so much. I think she bought it at Sears. You remember when Sears used to have that massive catalog? It was like a phone book. You could buy a whole house out of that thing back in the day, just order it and they'd send you the lumber on a train. \n\nBut yeah, I don't think I'm acting slower or anything. Just regular speed.\n\nDoctor: Do you often feel like you're worthless or that you blame yourself for things that happen?\n\nPatient: Worthless? No, I wouldn't say that. I mean, I do my job, I pay my bills. Blaming myself... not really. I mean, things just happen, you know? Like last week at the store, they had this huge display of those new LED work lights. Supposed to be super bright, like 5,000 lumens or something crazy. Anyway, some kid came running down the aisle and knocked the whole endcap over. Lights everywhere. The plastic packaging on those things is ridiculous, by the way. You need a chainsaw just to open them. Clamshell packaging, they call it. I cut my hand on one of those clamshells trying to open a pair of pliers a few months ago. Bled all over the counter. The manager made me fill out an incident report, like it was my fault the plastic was sharper than the actual pliers. \n\nThey use that same plastic for everything now. Batteries, scissors... you buy scissors, and you need scissors just to get them out of the package. It makes no sense. My grandfather used to have this old pair of heavy metal shears. Cast iron handles, heavy as a brick. He used them for everything—cutting wire, trimming the hedges, opening boxes. He kept them in this old leather tool belt that smelled like motor oil and peppermint. He always had peppermint candies in his pocket. The soft ones, not the hard discs. \n\nBut no, I don't sit around feeling worthless or blaming myself for stuff. It's just... life, you know?\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're having trouble getting motivated to do things that you normally do?\n\nPatient: Motivated... well, I don't know if it's motivation exactly. I mean, I go to work, I get up, but... I guess I'm just not doing the extra stuff. Like I said, the brewing and the records. But the everyday stuff, I'm still doing it. Barely, sometimes. I had to drag the trash cans out to the curb this morning, and the wheels on the recycling bin are completely shot. They get that grit in them from the road salt they put down in the winter, and it just grinds away at the plastic axle until it won't spin anymore. \n\nThe city is supposed to replace them if they break, but you have to call this hotline, and you're just on hold forever listening to this terrible elevator music. It sounds like a Casio keyboard from the 80s. My brother had one of those keyboards. He thought he was going to be the next Eddie Van Halen, but he only ever learned how to play the opening riff to \"Smoke on the Water.\" Over and over again, all summer long. Drove my dad absolutely up the wall. Dad finally took the batteries out and hid them in the garage behind his cans of WD-40. WD-40 is great, but people use it wrong, you know? It's a water displacer, not a true lubricant. If you really want to grease a squeaky hinge, you need white lithium grease. It comes in this little tube, looks just like toothpaste. \n\nBut yeah, motivation... I don't know. I'm just doing what I have to do to get by, I guess. It's just hard to care about the rest of it.\n\nDoctor: Are there any small things that you still find pleasure in or enjoy doing?\n\nPatient: Pleasure in small things? I mean... not really. That's the thing. I quit caring about the things that usually settle me down, you know? Like fishing, digging through vinyl records, tailgating, or checking on my home-brew. They all just felt like chores lately. I haven't even looked at my brewing setup in weeks. \n\nI used to love making this pale ale, used Cascade hops. You have to be careful with hops, though, because if you boil them too long, they get too bitter. My buddy Dave taught me that. Dave's a smart guy, works in HVAC. He came over last summer to fix my AC unit when it went out during that heatwave. Turns out it was just a blown capacitor. It's a little silver cylinder, looks like a tiny soda can. He swapped it out in ten minutes and only charged me for the part. \n\nHVAC guys make a killing, though. I should have learned a trade like that. Instead, I've been working retail for thirty years. You stand on that concrete all day, and it just ruins your back. I bought these expensive orthotic insoles, the kind you have to bake in the oven so they mold to your feet. They smelled terrible when I was heating them up, like burning tires. My wife made me open all the windows, and the pollen was so bad that week everything in the house got coated in this fine yellow dust. \n\nBut yeah, the brewing, the records... I just don't have the energy for it. It all just feels like work now.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're resting or sleeping more than usual?\n\nPatient: Sleeping more? I wish. Honestly, it's the opposite. Even when I do manage to fall asleep now, I wake up way before daylight. Pitch black outside, and my stomach is just tied in knots. My mind is already racing, thinking about bills and everything I've messed up lately. \n\nIt's so quiet at that time of morning, you know? You can hear every little creak in the house. The refrigerator kicks on, and it sounds like a jet engine. It's an older model, one of those side-by-sides with the ice maker in the door. The ice maker's been broken for five years, though. It just hums and clicks, but nothing comes out. I tried fixing it once, watched a bunch of videos online. They make it look so easy. \"Just unscrew this panel and replace the solenoid valve.\" Yeah, right. I ended up dropping a screw down the back of the freezer compartment, and it rattled around in the defrost fan for a week before I finally got it out with a magnet on a stick. \n\nMagnets are fascinating, really. My grandson has these magnetic building tiles, Magna-Tiles I think they're called. He builds these huge towers, and then just smashes them down. The noise drives my wife crazy, but he loves it. Kids have so much energy. I don't know where they get it. \n\nBut yeah, sleep... I'm definitely not getting more of it. Just lying there in the dark, stressing out before the sun even comes up.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like your movements or speech have slowed down or sped up compared to your normal pace?\n\nPatient: Slowed down? Or sped up? I don't... I don't really know. I haven't noticed anything like that, to be honest. I mean, my wife always tells me I talk too loud when I get going, but that's just because I spent so many years working in the stockroom before they moved me out to the floor. The conveyor belts back there are incredibly loud. It's this constant metal-on-metal screeching sound. \n\nThey finally replaced the rollers a couple of years ago, but before that, it was unbearable. The guy who came to fix them brought this huge toolbox, one of those red metal ones with all the drawers. Snap-on, I think. Those tools are so expensive. A single wrench can cost you fifty bucks. But I guess if you're using them every day, you want quality. Like shoes. I was telling you about those insoles... if you don't take care of your feet, your whole body pays for it. \n\nBut as for moving slow or fast... I don't think so. I'm just moving like I normally do, I guess. Nothing feels different about that.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like your body is heavier or harder to move than usual?\n\nPatient: Heavier? No, I wouldn't say that. I don't feel like I'm dragging weights around or anything. I mean, my knees ache, but that's just getting older, right? Fifty-four years of gravity taking its toll. \n\nGravity is a funny thing when you think about it. It just constantly pulls you down. I saw this documentary once about astronauts on the International Space Station, and how their muscles just waste away because they don't have gravity working against them. They have to strap themselves to these special treadmills with bungee cords just to get a workout in. Imagine running strapped down like that. Must chafe terribly. \n\nI bought a treadmill a few years ago, thinking I'd use it in the winter when it's too cold to walk outside. Found it on Craigslist for fifty bucks. The guy who sold it to me lived in this massive house out in the suburbs, had a three-car garage and everything. His driveway was paved with those stamped concrete blocks that look like cobblestones. I always wondered how they keep the weeds from growing up between all those cracks. My neighbor uses this vinegar and dish soap mixture on his driveway, swears it works better than Roundup. Plus, it's safer for the dogs. We used to have a golden retriever, Buster. Best dog in the world, but he chewed through the garden hose twice. \n\nBut no, my body doesn't feel heavier or harder to move. Just the usual aches and pains, I guess.\n\nDoctor: Have you had periods like this before where you felt similarly, or is this the first time?\n\nPatient: Periods like this? You mean... feeling like this? I don't know, maybe? It's hard to remember. I feel like I've always been a worrier, you know? Like my mind is always going. But this bad... I'm not sure. \n\nMy memory isn't what it used to be. I was trying to remember the name of this actor the other day, the one who was in that movie with the boat... not Titanic, the other one. The one with the giant shark. Jaws! Right. Richard Dreyfuss. I loved him in Mr. Holland's Opus. Such a good movie. We had it on VHS. Remember VHS tapes? You had to rewind them before you took them back to Blockbuster or they'd charge you a fee. \"Be kind, rewind.\" My kids don't even know what a VHS tape is. Everything is streaming now. Netflix, Hulu, Disney Plus... it's too much. We pay more for all these subscriptions than we ever did for cable. \n\nI tried to cancel our cable last month, actually. Called the company, and they kept transferring me to different departments. \"Retention specialists,\" they call them. They just read from a script and offer you a discount for six months, hoping you'll forget to cancel again when the price goes back up. It's a racket, really. Just like those extended warranties they try to sell you at the electronics store. Complete waste of money. \n\nBut yeah... have I felt like this before? I honestly couldn't tell you. It all just kind of blends together after a while.\n\nDoctor: How long have you been feeling this low, is it just these three months or has it been longer?\n\nPatient: Three months. Yeah, it's been about three months. Ever since that... that crash, you know? Just this low spell hanging on since then. \n\nIt's funny how time works, though. Three months can feel like a lifetime or just a blink. Like when I was working the holiday rush at the store last year. November and December just evaporated. Poof. Gone. We were selling these remote-control drones, right? They were the hot item. Every parent came in looking for one, and we kept running out of stock. The shipping delays were a nightmare. The manager, Gary—he's this guy who wears those ties with the Looney Tunes characters on them, never quite matched his shirts—Gary was pulling his hair out. He actually has a bald spot right on the crown of his head that he tries to cover up, but the fluorescent lights in the store just bounce right off it. \n\nFluorescent lighting is terrible for you, by the way. I read an article that said it messes with your circadian rhythm. That's why I swapped all the bulbs in our house to those warm LED ones. Cost a small fortune, but they last forever. Supposedly. Although I had one burn out in the hallway fixture after just a month. Took it right back to the hardware store with the receipt. You always have to keep the receipt. My dad taught me that. He had this old shoebox in his closet where he kept every receipt for everything he ever bought. Even a pack of gum. \n\nBut yeah, it's been about three months of feeling this low. Since the crash.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever felt this low before, or is this the first time in your life?\n\nPatient: First time? You know, I... I don't really know. Like I said before, it's hard to remember. It all kind of blends together. I mean, I've had times where I felt down, sure. Who hasn't? But this low? I'm not sure. \n\nIt's like trying to remember what the weather was like on this exact day five years ago. You just can't do it unless you wrote it down. My wife, she keeps these little daily planner books. Has a stack of them in the bottom drawer of her nightstand going back to like 1998. She writes everything down. Dentist appointments, when she watered the houseplants, what we had for dinner. Last week she made this meatloaf with a brown sugar glaze on top. It was okay, but I prefer the classic ketchup glaze. That's how my mom used to make it. She'd mix a little mustard into the ketchup before she spread it on. Gives it a nice tang, you know? \n\nMustard is one of those things, people either love it or hate it. Like mayonnaise. I can't stand mayonnaise. The texture is just wrong. I went to this deli once and specifically asked for no mayo on my turkey sandwich, and what do they do? Slathered it on both pieces of bread. I had to scrape it off with a plastic knife. Those plastic knives are useless, though. They bend if you press too hard. You can't even cut a pickle with them. \n\nBut yeah, feeling this low before... I really couldn't say for sure. Maybe? Or maybe this is the first time it's been this bad. I just don't know."
